\begin{document}
\thispagestyle{opening}
\begin{center}
{\LARGE\sffamily\bfseries\color{parttitle}The $\grp{2C_{\infty h}}$ subgroup of $\grp{Pin^{-}(3)}$}
\end{center}
\contentspage
\needspace{194pt}
\partbox{The group}
\section{Names and structure}\label{s:identification}
Conventions: \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, sections \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.0.1}{0.1}, \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.2}{2}, \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.12}{12}, \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.20}{20} and \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.29}{29}.
\label{n:mark.classes}{\small\begin{longtable}{@{}ll@{}}\toprule
\texttt{symbol} & \href{2Cinfh.pdf}{$\grp{2C_{\infty h}}$}\\
\texttt{cover\symbol{95}of} & \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/Cinfh.pdf}{$\grp{C_{\infty h}}$}\\
\grouprule{0pt}{\lightrulewidth}
\texttt{abstract\symbol{95}type} & $\grp{SO(2)}\dirprod \Cyc{2}$\\
\grouprule{0pt}{\lightrulewidth}
\texttt{su2\symbol{95}subgroup} & \href{2Cinf.pdf}{$\grp{2C_{\infty }}$}, index 2\\
\texttt{normalizer} & \href{2Dinfh.pdf}{$\grp{2D_{\infty h}}$}, index 2\\
\texttt{centralizer} & \href{2Cinfh.pdf}{$\grp{2C_{\infty h}}$}, index 2 in the normalizer\\
\texttt{product\symbol{95}form} & \begin{tabular}[t]{@{}l@{}}direct: \href{2Cinf.pdf}{$\grp{2C_{\infty }}$}${}\dirprod{}$\href{S2.pdf}{$\grp{C_{i}}$}\metclasses{\cls{E}, \cls{\lift{i}}}; \href{2Cinf.pdf}{$\grp{2C_{\infty }}$}${}\dirprod{}$\href{S2b.pdf}{$\grp{C_{i}}\secondclass$}\metclasses{\cls{E}, \cls{\barred{\lift{i}}}}\end{tabular}\\
\texttt{center} & \href{2Cinfh.pdf}{$\grp{2C_{\infty h}}$}, index 1\\
\texttt{commutator\symbol{95}subgroup} & \href{C1.pdf}{$\grp{C_{1}}$}\\
\grouprule{0pt}{\lightrulewidth}
\texttt{alternative\symbol{95}names} & \href{2Cinfh.pdf}{$\grp{2S_{\infty }}$}\\
\texttt{hermann\symbol{95}mauguin} & $\dmark\hmname{\infty /m}$\\
\bottomrule\end{longtable}}
\leavevmode\label{n:group.preimage}The group $\grp{2C_{\infty h}}$ is the preimage in $\grp{Pin^{-}(3)}$ of \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/Cinfh.pdf}{$\grp{C_{\infty h}}$}, whose document sets the fields that this table does not repeat (\texttt{cover\symbol{95}of}): its type, family, geometry, crystal classes and the names of its \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/O3.pdf}{$\grp{O(3)}$}-conjugacy class. This preimage is continuous, so its order and GAP identifier are undefined. The field \texttt{su2\symbol{95}subgroup} is $\grp{2C_{\infty h}} \cap \grp{SU(2)}$, the largest subgroup of $\grp{2C_{\infty h}}$ in $\grp{SU(2)}$ (its elements of parity $+1$), and \texttt{normalizer} and \texttt{centralizer} are taken in $\grp{Pin^{-}(3)}$.
In \texttt{product\symbol{95}form}, the braces contain the classes of $\grp{2C_{\infty h}}$ that the finite second factor meets: atoms of the class space (section~\ref{s:class_space}) and distinguished points of its arcs (\href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, sections \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.16}{16}, \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.21}{21} and \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.29.1}{29.1}).
\needspace{103pt}
\section{Class space}\label{s:class_space}
Conventions: \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, sections \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.16}{16}, \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.21}{21} and \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.29.1}{29.1}.
\leavevmode\label{n:head.component}A row is a component of the class space of $\grp{2C_{\infty h}}$, an arc of classes. \label{n:head.coset}\label{n:head.element}A row gives whether its elements cover proper or improper elements of \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/Cinfh.pdf}{$\grp{C_{\infty h}}$} (coset) and the element of $\grp{Pin^{-}(3)}$ at its angle. \label{n:angle.omega}The elements at the angles are the lift $\lift{R}_z(\omega) = \cos\tfrac{\omega}{2} - \iunit\sin\tfrac{\omega}{2}\,\pauli{z}$, $0 \le \omega < 4\pi$, and its products $\elt{\lift{i}}\,\lift{R}_z(\omega)$ with the lift of the inversion $\elt{\lift{i}}$ (\href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, section \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.29.1}{29.1}). \label{n:matrix.element.pin}The matrix $M(g)$ of the element is the transpose of its $\grp{SU(2)}$ part, so that the matrices compose as the matrices $P(g)$ of the elements of \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/Cinfh.pdf}{$\grp{C_{\infty h}}$} (\href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, section \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.21.2}{21.2}).
The constant $\iunit$ is the imaginary unit, and $\expe$ is the base of the natural logarithm.
\begin{center}\begin{tabular}{@{}llll@{}}\toprule
component & coset & element & $M(g)$\\\midrule
$\comp{\lift{C}(\omega)}$ & proper & $\elt{\lift{C}}(\omega)$ & $\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\expe^{-\iunit\omega/2} & 0\\ 0 & \expe^{\iunit\omega/2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)$\\\addlinespace[6pt]
$\comp{\lift{i}\,\lift{C}(\omega)}$ & improper & $\elt{\lift{i}\,\lift{C}}(\omega)$ & $\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\expe^{-\iunit\omega/2} & 0\\ 0 & \expe^{\iunit\omega/2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)$\\
\bottomrule\end{tabular}\end{center}
The group $\grp{2C_{\infty h}}$ is abelian, so every element is a class, and the arcs run over $0 \le \omega < 4\pi$, where the two lifts $\omega$ and $\omega + 2\pi$ of a rotation lie apart.
The distinguished points are $\cls{E}$ at $\omega = 0$, $\cls{C_2}$ at $\omega = \pi$, $\cls{\barred{E}}$ at $\omega = 2\pi$, $\cls{\barred{C}_2}$ at $\omega = 3\pi$, $\cls{\lift{i}}$ at $\omega = 0$, $\cls{\sigma_h}$ at $\omega = \pi$, $\cls{\barred{\lift{i}}}$ at $\omega = 2\pi$ and $\cls{\barred{\sigma}_h}$ at $\omega = 3\pi$.
\leavevmode\label{n:local.haar.h}The Haar measure, of total mass 1, is $\diff h = \diff\omega/(8\pi)$ on $\comp{\lift{C}(\omega)}$, $\omega\in\bigl[0, 4\pi\bigr)$, of mass $\tfrac{1}{2}$, and $\diff h = \diff\omega/(8\pi)$ on $\comp{\lift{i}\,\lift{C}(\omega)}$, $\omega\in\bigl[0, 4\pi\bigr)$, of mass $\tfrac{1}{2}$.
\needspace{103pt}
\section{Fundamental domain}\label{s:fundamental_domain}
Conventions: \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, sections \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.17}{17} and \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.21.3}{21.3}.
An object with symmetry $\grp{2C_{\infty h}}$ placed by $\lift{Q} \in \grp{Pin^{-}(3)}$ is the same when placed by $g\lift{Q}$ with $g \in \grp{2C_{\infty h}}$. \label{n:operator.cosets}Thus its orientations are the cosets $\grp{2C_{\infty h}}\lift{Q} = \{g\lift{Q} : g \in \grp{2C_{\infty h}}\}$, the points of the space $\grp{2C_{\infty h}}\backslash\grp{Pin^{-}(3)}$. \label{n:operator.volume}Volumes are those of the metric that the covering map carries to that of \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/O3.pdf}{$\grp{O(3)}$} (\href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, section \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.17}{17}), so that $\vol\grp{SU(2)} = 16\pi^2$ and $\vol\grp{Pin^{-}(3)} = 32\pi^2$, twice those of $\grp{SO(3)}$ and $\grp{O(3)}$.
\leavevmode\label{n:set.sphere}As $\grp{2C_{\infty h}}$ contains $\elt{\barred{E}}$, a coset $\grp{2C_{\infty h}}\lift{Q}$ contains both lifts of every element of a coset of \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/Cinfh.pdf}{$\grp{C_{\infty h}}$} in $\grp{O(3)}$, and $\grp{2C_{\infty h}}\backslash\grp{Pin^{-}(3)}$ is $\grp{C_{\infty h}}\backslash\grp{O(3)}$, $\grp{C_{\infty}}\backslash\grp{SO(3)}$, the sphere $\twosphere$. A cell's rotations stand here for their canonical lifts (\href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, section \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.21.1}{21.1}). \label{n:set.cell}\label{n:set.fundamental}Thus the cell $\fcell$ and the fundamental domain $\fdomain$ are those of the document of \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/Cinfh.pdf}{$\grp{C_{\infty h}}$}, in its section Fundamental domain. The group $\grp{2C_{\infty h}}$ covers \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/Cinfh.pdf}{$\grp{C_{\infty h}}$} twice, and the circles of its components have length $4\pi$, so that $\vol\grp{2C_{\infty h}} = 8\pi$. Hence
\begin{equation}\label{e:volume}\vol\fdomain = 4\pi,\qquad \vol\grp{2C_{\infty h}}\cdot\vol\fdomain = 8\pi\cdot 4\pi = 32\pi^2 = \vol\grp{Pin^{-}(3)}.\end{equation}

The group $\grp{2C_{\infty h}}$ acts on the directions through the covering map, so its orbits are those of the document of \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/Cinfh.pdf}{$\grp{C_{\infty h}}$} (\texttt{orbit\symbol{95}space}).
The harmonics on $\grp{2C_{\infty h}}\backslash\grp{Pin^{-}(3)}$ are those on $\grp{C_{\infty h}}\backslash\grp{O(3)}$ of the document of \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/Cinfh.pdf}{$\grp{C_{\infty h}}$} (\texttt{harmonics}), of integer $J$ since $\elt{\barred{E}}$ acts on $\irrJp{J}{p}$ (\href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, section \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.25.1}{25.1}) by $(-1)^{2J}$.
\needspace{171pt}
\partbox{Representations}
\section{Families of irreps}\label{s:families}
Conventions: \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, sections \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.7}{7}, \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.16}{16}, \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.23}{23} and \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.29.2}{29.2}.
\leavevmode\label{n:head.family.irreps}\label{n:index.dimension}\label{n:index.frobenius}\label{n:head.index.family}A row is a family of irreps, by its label, with its dimension $d$, its Frobenius--Schur indicator $\fsind$ (real for $+1$ and complex for $0$) and the range of the index of its irreps. \label{n:head.conjugate}\label{n:head.over.real}It also gives the family of the irreps over $\mathbb{R}$ in which its irreps lie and the family of the complex-conjugate irreps. \label{n:head.kernel}\label{n:function.character}The kernel $\ker\Gamma$ is the subgroup of the elements that an irrep $\Gamma$ of the family maps to the identity, and the character of $\Gamma$ at $g$ is $\irchar{\Gamma}(g)$. \label{n:head.image}The image is the group formed by the irrep's matrices, isomorphic to $\grp{2C_{\infty h}}/\ker\Gamma$. A row also gives the family's character on each component, in the angle $\omega$ of the lift. A dash stands for none. The vector families, on which $\elt{\barred{E}}$ is the identity, are those of \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/Cinfh.pdf}{$\grp{C_{\infty h}}$} (\texttt{vector\symbol{95}families}), read on these classes through the covering map. A point $\omega$ of an arc covers the rotation angle $\phi = \omega$ modulo $2\pi$. The image and matrices of a vector family are those of \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/Cinfh.pdf}{$\grp{C_{\infty h}}$}, and its kernel is the preimage of its kernel in that group. The spinor families, on which $\elt{\barred{E}}$ is $-1$, follow below the rule, with an instance for every half-integer $n = \tfrac{1}{2}, \tfrac{3}{2}, \dots$. The image of a spinor family is \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/Cinf.pdf}{$\grp{C_{\infty}}$}, the group of the characters, turning the complex plane. As $2n$ is odd, the kernel of a spinor instance $n$ is a section (\href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, section \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.27}{27}). The irreps are irreducible over $\mathbb{C}$. Over $\mathbb{R}$ (\href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, sections \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.6.4}{6.4}, \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.7}{7} and \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.23.2}{23.2}), a real irrep is also an irrep in a basis with real matrices. The two irreps $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}$, $\conjpair{2}\irr{e}$ of a complex-conjugate pair together form one irrep over $\mathbb{R}$ of dimension $2d$ and character $2\operatorname{Re}\irchar{\conjpair{1}\irr{e}}$, labeled without the prefix when they are vector irreps. A complex-conjugate pair of spinor irreps forms one irrep over $\mathbb{R}$ of dimension $2d$, named by the labels of its two members side by side, in irrep order. \label{n:mark.determinant}The boxed family, of one irrep, is the determinant character of \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/Cinfh.pdf}{$\grp{C_{\infty h}}$}, of value $\det g$ on the elements that cover $g$.

The record holds the spinor instances up to $n = 13/2$, with their characters at the distinguished points (\texttt{instances}).

\section{Representation matrices}\label{s:continuous_irreps}
Conventions: \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, sections \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.9}{9}, \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.16}{16}, \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.23.3}{23.3} and \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.29.2}{29.2}.
\leavevmode\label{n:vector.ket.partner}\label{n:index.partner}\label{n:matrix.irrep}A basis $(\irket{\Gamma}{1}, \dots, \irket{\Gamma}{d})$ of the irrep $\Gamma$ obeys
\begin{equation}\label{e:partner}g\irket{\Gamma}{\intx} = \sum_\nu \irket{\Gamma}{\nu}\, \repD{\Gamma}_{\nu,\intx}(g);\end{equation}
the matrices of a vector family are those of \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/Cinfh.pdf}{$\grp{C_{\infty h}}$}, on the elements that cover its elements. The spinor irreps form families. A row of a table gives $\repD{\Gamma}(g)$ of a spinor family for the elements of a component of the class space (section~\ref{s:class_space}), as a function of their angle.
For a family of parity $p$, the matrix $\repD{\Gamma}(g) = \irchar{\Gamma}(g)$ acts on one ket of $\irrJp{n}{p}$, $\jm{n}{n}$ for a family $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}$ and $\jm{n}{-n}$ for a family $\conjpair{2}\irr{e}$.

\begin{longtable}{@{}lllll@{}}\toprule
$g$ & $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{n,\ptag{g}}$ & $\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{n,\ptag{g}}$ & $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{n,\ptag{u}}$ & $\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{n,\ptag{u}}$\\\midrule\endhead
\bottomrule\endfoot\endlastfoot
$\elt{\lift{C}}(\omega)$ & $\expe^{\iunit n\omega}$ & $\expe^{-\iunit n\omega}$ & $\expe^{\iunit n\omega}$ & $\expe^{-\iunit n\omega}$\\*

\section{Direct products of irreps}\label{s:fusion}
Conventions: \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, sections \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.10.2}{10.2} and \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.29.3}{29.3}.
\leavevmode\label{n:index.family}A rule is listed for every pair of families, of indices $n$ and $n'$, by the kinds of the factors, where a vector index is an integer and a spinor index a half-integer. The squares of the families follow, with the antisymmetric part in braces. The rules with two vector factors are those of \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/Cinfh.pdf}{$\grp{C_{\infty h}}$} (\texttt{vector\symbol{95}rules}).

\needspace{78pt}
\section{Clebsch--Gordan coefficients}\label{s:clebsch_gordan}
Conventions: \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, sections \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.10.3}{10.3} and \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.29.3}{29.3}.
Every irrep has dimension 1: the characters multiply, and the coefficient has the value 1. The $\ptag{g}$ or $\ptag{u}$ of the result is the product of those of the factors. The products of two vector irreps have the coefficients of \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/Cinfh.pdf}{$\grp{C_{\infty h}}$} (\texttt{vector\symbol{95}products}).
\setlength{\emergencystretch}{2em}\par\setlength{\emergencystretch}{0pt}
\needspace{171pt}
\partbox{Subgroups}
\section{Closed subgroups}\label{s:subgroups}
Conventions: \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, sections \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.16.1}{16.1} and \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.29.5}{29.5}.
The closed subgroups of $\grp{2C_{\infty h}}$ up to conjugacy in it are the preimages of the closed subgroups of \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/Cinfh.pdf}{$\grp{C_{\infty h}}$} (in the section Closed subgroups of the covered group's document) and the sections.
\leavevmode\label{n:index.family.N}Some members with $N = 1$ have other names: \href{2S2.pdf}{$\grp{2S_{2}}$} $\samecls$ \href{2S2.pdf}{$\grp{2C_{i}}$} and \href{2C1h.pdf}{$\grp{2C_{1h}}$} $\samecls$ \href{2C1v.pdf}{$\grp{2C_{s}}$}.
In the diagram, each group links to its document, and a line joins a group to a maximal closed subgroup among the groups of the diagram. The groups that do not lie in $\grp{2C_{\infty h}}$ are gray.

\leavevmode\label{n:group.H}The table lists the closed subgroups $\grp{H}$ other than $\grp{2C_{\infty h}}$ up to conjugacy in $\grp{2C_{\infty h}}$, one in each row: the continuous subgroups, then the preimages and the sections of each family. Members of different families are not conjugate in $\grp{2C_{\infty h}}$. A row with $N$ in its symbol stands for every $N$ of its range, and its entries are functions of $N$. \label{n:head.subgroup}\label{n:head.order}\label{n:head.conjugates}\label{n:head.normal}A row gives the representative $\grp{H}$ of a class of subgroups (subgrp), its order, the number of subgroups in the class (conjugates) and whether $\grp{H}$ is normal. \label{n:group.normalizer}\label{n:group.centralizer}It also gives the normalizer $\grp{N}_{\hairsp \grp{2C_{\infty h}}}(\grp{H})$ and the centralizer $\grp{C}_{\hairsp \grp{2C_{\infty h}}}(\grp{H})$ in $\grp{2C_{\infty h}}$ (\texttt{normalizer}, \texttt{centralizer}). \label{n:head.components}The column components gives the components of the class space (section~\ref{s:class_space}) met by the subgroup. The record holds the members with $N \le 12$ (\texttt{subduction\symbol{95}subgroups}). A subgroup's other names in its \href{Pinminus3.pdf}{$\grp{Pin^{-}(3)}$}-conjugacy class follow $\samecls$ (\href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, section \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.20.2}{20.2}).

\needspace{203pt}
\section{Descent of symmetry}\label{s:subduction}
Conventions: \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, sections \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.16.1}{16.1} and \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.29.5}{29.5}.
\needspace{149pt}
\paragraph{Vector families.} The vector families of $\grp{2C_{\infty h}}$ are those of \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/Cinfh.pdf}{$\grp{C_{\infty h}}$}. They restrict to the preimage $\grp{2H}$ of a closed subgroup $\grp{H}$ of \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/Cinfh.pdf}{$\grp{C_{\infty h}}$} as \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/Cinfh.pdf}{$\grp{C_{\infty h}}$} restricts them to $\grp{H}$ (\texttt{vector\symbol{95}entry}, \texttt{vector\symbol{95}rules}), whose irreps are the vector irreps of $\grp{2H}$. The families restrict to a section as to its preimage. The tables are those of the section Descent of symmetry of the document of \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/Cinfh.pdf}{$\grp{C_{\infty h}}$}, with its settings. They are headed by the preimages and give the images of the generators of $\grp{H}$ at the points of the class space (section~\ref{s:class_space}). Members of different families are not conjugate in $\grp{2C_{\infty h}}$. \label{n:irrep.Gamma.subgroup}The entry of row $\Gamma$ is $\restr{\Gamma}{\grp{H}}$ as a sum of the irreps of $\grp{H}$.
{\small\begin{longtable}{@{}lc@{}}\toprule
subgrp & \href{2Cinf.pdf}{$\grp{2C_{\infty }}$}\\
index & 2\\\midrule\endhead
\bottomrule\endfoot\endlastfoot

$\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{n,\ptag{g}}$ & $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{n}$\\*
$\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{n,\ptag{g}}$ & $\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{n}$\\*

\bottomrule\end{longtable}}

\needspace{125pt}
\paragraph{Spinor families.} Below is the restriction of the spinor families to the continuous subgroups and to the finite subgroups that the rules and the tables list. The first table gives it for each continuous subgroup $\grp{H}$, and the row index gives its index $\grpindex{\grp{2C_{\infty h}}}{\grp{H}}$.

\leavevmode\label{n:index.residue}For the preimage $\grp{2C_{\mathnormal{N}}}$, the rules give it for every $N$ by $q$ ($0 < q < N$):
{\small\begin{longtable}{@{}ll>{\RaggedRight\arraybackslash}p{0.7\textwidth}@{}}\toprule
subgrp & family & irreps\\\midrule\endhead
\bottomrule\endfoot\endlastfoot
$\grp{2C_{\mathnormal{N}}}$ & $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{n,\ptag{g}}$ & $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{q}$ if $q < N/2$, $\irr{a}_{N/2}$ if $q = N/2$, $\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{N-q}$ if $q > N/2$; $q = n \bmod N$\\*
$\grp{2C_{\mathnormal{N}}}$ & $\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{n,\ptag{g}}$ & $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{q}$ if $q < N/2$, $\irr{a}_{N/2}$ if $q = N/2$, $\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{N-q}$ if $q > N/2$; $q = -n \bmod N$\\
$\grp{2C_{\mathnormal{N}}}$ & $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{n,\ptag{u}}$ & $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{q}$ if $q < N/2$, $\irr{a}_{N/2}$ if $q = N/2$, $\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{N-q}$ if $q > N/2$; $q = n \bmod N$\\*

\bottomrule\end{longtable}}
\leavevmode\label{n:index.period}For a finite subgroup the restriction of the instance $n$ depends on $n$ only modulo its period $\period$. The table lists the finite preimages with $N \le 12$ and gives, for $n = \tfrac{1}{2}, \tfrac{3}{2}, \dots, \period - \tfrac{1}{2}$, the irreps of the subgroup, and the record holds the intertwiners (\texttt{blocks}). A section is restricted as its preimage, with each spinor irrep renamed by the section's record (\href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, section \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.27}{27}).

\needspace{21pt}
\paragraph{Induction.} \label{n:irrep.Delta}For a finite subgroup $\grp{H}$, $\Ind{\grp{H}}{\grp{2C_{\infty h}}} \Lambda$ has infinite dimension: by Frobenius reciprocity it contains each irrep of $\grp{2C_{\infty h}}$ as often as its restriction to $\grp{H}$ contains $\Lambda$ (\href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, section \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.12.4}{12.4}).
\needspace{177pt}
\partbox{Angular momentum}
\section{Subduction from \texorpdfstring{$\grp{Pin^{-}(3)}$}{Pin\unichar{8315}(3)}}\label{s:subduction_parent}
Conventions: \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, sections \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.11}{11}, \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.25.1}{25.1} and \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.29.4}{29.4}.
\leavevmode\label{n:irrep.o3}\label{n:index.J}\label{n:index.parity}The irrep $\irrJp{J}{p}$ of \href{Pinminus3.pdf}{$\grp{Pin^{-}(3)}$} at half-integer $J$ restricts to $\grp{2C_{\infty h}}$ by the rule (\texttt{rule}), at $p = +1$:
\begin{equation}\label{e:parent}\restr{\irrJp{J}{+1}}{\grp{2C_{\infty h}}} = \bigoplus_{n=1/2}^{J}(\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{n,\ptag{g}}\oplus\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{n,\ptag{g}}),\end{equation}
where the sum runs over $n = \tfrac{1}{2}, \tfrac{3}{2}, \dots, J$, and $\irrJp{J}{-1}$ restricts to the same irreps with $\ptag{u}$ for $\ptag{g}$. At integer $J$ the restriction is the subduction of \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/Cinfh.pdf}{$\grp{C_{\infty h}}$} (\texttt{integer\symbol{95}J}).
\needspace{110pt}
\section{Multiplicities and allowed \texorpdfstring{$m$}{m}}\label{s:multiplicities}
Conventions: \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, sections \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.14}{14}, \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.26.2}{26.2} and \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.29.4}{29.4}.
\leavevmode\label{n:function.generating}\label{n:index.generating.variable}The multiplicity $b_\Gamma(J)$ is the number of blocks of $\Gamma$ at $J$, with the generating function
\begin{equation}\label{e:gf}H_\Gamma(t) = \sum_{J\ge1/2} b_\Gamma(J)\,t^{2J},\end{equation}
for each family's instance of index $n$, at the parity $p$ of $\irrJp{J}{p}$ carried by the kets (\texttt{generating\symbol{95}function}). The bracket $\iverson{n\le J}$ is 1 when $n \le J$ and 0 otherwise. The column $m$ gives the $m$ of the kets of $\Gamma$ (\texttt{allowed\symbol{95}m}).
\begin{center}\small{\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}\begin{tabular}{@{}lllll@{}}\toprule
family & $H_\Gamma(t)$ & $b_\Gamma(J)$, $p = +1$ & $p = -1$ & $m$\\\midrule
$\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{n,\ptag{g}}$ & $\dfrac{t^{2n}}{1-t^{2}}$ & $\iverson{n\le J}$ & $0$ & $n$\\
$\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{n,\ptag{g}}$ & $\dfrac{t^{2n}}{1-t^{2}}$ & $\iverson{n\le J}$ & $0$ & $-n$\\
$\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{n,\ptag{u}}$ & $\dfrac{t^{2n}}{1-t^{2}}$ & $0$ & $\iverson{n\le J}$ & $n$\\
$\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{n,\ptag{u}}$ & $\dfrac{t^{2n}}{1-t^{2}}$ & $0$ & $\iverson{n\le J}$ & $-n$\\
\bottomrule\end{tabular}}\end{center}
\needspace{91pt}
\section{Symmetry-adapted bases}\label{s:bases}
Conventions: \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, sections \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.14}{14}, \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.26.2}{26.2} and \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.29.4}{29.4}.
\leavevmode\label{n:index.m}The bases at integer $J$ are those of \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/Cinfh.pdf}{$\grp{C_{\infty h}}$} (\texttt{integer\symbol{95}J}), and at half-integer $J$ the kets $\jm{J}{m}$ carry $\irrJp{J}{+1}$. Every ket $\jm{J}{m}$ is a block, of $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{m,\ptag{g}}$ for $m > 0$ and of $\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{-m,\ptag{g}}$ for $m < 0$. At $p = -1$ a block keeps its kets and partners, and the $\ptag{g}$ of its irrep becomes $\ptag{u}$. The record holds the blocks for $2J \le 21$ (\texttt{enumerated}).
\setlength{\emergencystretch}{2em}\par\setlength{\emergencystretch}{0pt}
\needspace{171pt}
\partbox{Spinors and tensors}
\section{Spin--orbitals}\label{s:spin_orbitals}
Conventions: \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, section \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.25.4}{25.4}.
\leavevmode\label{n:index.orbital.l}The states of an electron of orbital angular momentum $l$ and spin $\nicefrac{1}{2}$ are each given up to a positive factor. \label{n:local.spinor.uv}\label{n:local.function.f}They are written in the functions $f$ of the $\orbital{s}$, $\orbital{p}$ and $\orbital{d}$ shells of \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/Cinfh.pdf}{$\grp{C_{\infty h}}$} (\href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, section \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.13}{13}) times the spin kets $u = \jm{1/2}{1/2}$ and $v = \jm{1/2}{-1/2}$. The record also holds the $\orbital{f}$ shell (\texttt{spin\symbol{95}orbitals}). An orbital level consists of the functions of degree $l$ that carry the instance $\Gamma$ of \href{../../../rotation/data/pdf/Cinfh.pdf}{$\grp{C_{\infty h}}$}. With the spin, it splits into the spinor instances $\Lambda$ of $\grp{2C_{\infty h}}$, those of the doublet of $\Gamma$ in the spin--orbit levels (section~\ref{s:spin_orbit}, first column). \label{n:head.state}Under the heading of its shell, a row gives $\Gamma$, $\Lambda$, its partner $\intx$ and the state. The normalized state (\href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, section \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.25.4}{25.4}) follows the partner law with the matrices of section~\ref{s:continuous_irreps}.
{\small\begin{longtable}{@{}llrl@{\hspace{18pt}}llrl@{}}\toprule
$\Gamma$ & $\Lambda$ & $\intx$ & state & $\Gamma$ & $\Lambda$ & $\intx$ & state\\\midrule\endhead
\bottomrule\endfoot\endlastfoot
\multicolumn{4}{@{}l@{\hspace{18pt}}}{$\orbital{s}$ shell} & \multicolumn{4}{@{}l@{}}{$\orbital{d}$ shell}\\*
$\irr{a}_{\ptag{g}}$ & $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{1/2,\ptag{g}}$ & 1 & $\orbital{s}u$ & $\irr{a}_{\ptag{g}}$ & $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{1/2,\ptag{g}}$ & 1 & $\orbital{d}_{z^2}u$\\*
 & $\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{1/2,\ptag{g}}$ & 1 & $\orbital{s}v$ &  & $\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{1/2,\ptag{g}}$ & 1 & $\orbital{d}_{z^2}v$\\*
\multicolumn{4}{@{}l@{\hspace{18pt}}}{$\orbital{p}$ shell} & $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{1\ptag{g}}$ & $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{1/2,\ptag{g}}$ & 1 & $\left(\orbital{d}_{yz} - \iunit{}\,\orbital{d}_{zx}\right)v$\\*
$\irr{a}_{\ptag{u}}$ & $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{1/2,\ptag{u}}$ & 1 & $\orbital{p}_{z}u$ &  & $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{3/2,\ptag{g}}$ & 1 & $\left(\orbital{d}_{yz} - \iunit{}\,\orbital{d}_{zx}\right)u$\\*
 & $\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{1/2,\ptag{u}}$ & 1 & $\orbital{p}_{z}v$ & $\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{1\ptag{g}}$ & $\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{1/2,\ptag{g}}$ & 1 & $\left(\orbital{d}_{yz} + \iunit{}\,\orbital{d}_{zx}\right)u$\\*
$\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{1\ptag{u}}$ & $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{1/2,\ptag{u}}$ & 1 & $\left(\orbital{p}_{x} + \iunit{}\,\orbital{p}_{y}\right)v$ &  & $\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{3/2,\ptag{g}}$ & 1 & $\left(\orbital{d}_{yz} + \iunit{}\,\orbital{d}_{zx}\right)v$\\*
 & $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{3/2,\ptag{u}}$ & 1 & $\left(\orbital{p}_{x} + \iunit{}\,\orbital{p}_{y}\right)u$ & $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{2\ptag{g}}$ & $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{3/2,\ptag{g}}$ & 1 & $\left(\orbital{d}_{x^2-y^2} + 2\iunit{}\,\orbital{d}_{xy}\right)v$\\*
$\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{1\ptag{u}}$ & $\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{1/2,\ptag{u}}$ & 1 & $\left(\orbital{p}_{x} - \iunit{}\,\orbital{p}_{y}\right)u$ &  & $\conjpair{1}\irr{e}_{5/2,\ptag{g}}$ & 1 & $\left(\orbital{d}_{x^2-y^2} + 2\iunit{}\,\orbital{d}_{xy}\right)u$\\*
 & $\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{3/2,\ptag{u}}$ & 1 & $\left(\orbital{p}_{x} - \iunit{}\,\orbital{p}_{y}\right)v$ & $\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{2\ptag{g}}$ & $\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{3/2,\ptag{g}}$ & 1 & $\left(\orbital{d}_{x^2-y^2} - 2\iunit{}\,\orbital{d}_{xy}\right)u$\\*
 &  &  &  &  & $\conjpair{2}\irr{e}_{5/2,\ptag{g}}$ & 1 & $\left(\orbital{d}_{x^2-y^2} - 2\iunit{}\,\orbital{d}_{xy}\right)v$\\

\needspace{103pt}
\section{Invariant polynomials}\label{s:invariants}
Conventions: \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, section \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.26.4}{26.4}.
\leavevmode\label{n:local.binary.form}A binary form $f = \sum_i c_i\, u^i v^{n-i}$ of degree $n$ in the spin kets $u = \jm{1/2}{1/2}$ and $v = \jm{1/2}{-1/2}$ is invariant when every element leaves it unchanged, the elements acting at the parity $+1$ (\href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, section \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.26.4}{26.4}). At the parity $-1$ the invariant forms are the same, all of even degree. The matrices of the elements on $u$, $v$ are the diagonal matrices of $\grp{SU(2)}$, $u \mapsto \expe^{\iunit\omega/2}u$, $v \mapsto \expe^{-\iunit\omega/2}v$, so the invariant forms are the polynomials in $uv$. The power $(uv)^J$ is $J!$ times the ket $\jm{J}{0}$, whose diagonal matrix element is a Legendre polynomial [\href{../../../REFERENCES.pdf\#ref.VK91}{VK91}, section 6.3.11].
\leavevmode\label{n:function.hilbert}Every spinor irrep $V$ of $\grp{2C_{\infty h}}$ has dimension 1. \label{n:local.partner.x}Its values on the lifts of the rotations about $z$ are all the complex numbers of modulus 1, so no polynomial of positive degree in its partner $x$ is invariant. The number of linearly independent invariant polynomials of degree $n$ in the partner is the coefficient of $t^n$ in the Molien series $H_V(t)$ [\href{../../../REFERENCES.pdf\#ref.Mol97}{Mol97}]. This number is the multiplicity of the trivial irrep in the $n$-th symmetric power of $V$. Thus $H_V(t) = 1$. A level of section~\ref{s:jj}, a pair of complex-conjugate spinor irreps of partners $x$ and $y$, has the polynomials in $xy$, and $H_V(t) = 1/(1 - t^{2})$ (\texttt{invariants.molien}).
\needspace{177pt}
\partbox{Electronic levels}
\section{Spin--orbit levels}\label{s:spin_orbit}
Conventions: \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, sections \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.19}{19}, \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.25.2}{25.2} and \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.29.4}{29.4}.
\leavevmode\label{n:local.spin}\label{n:symbol.term}A term $\term{2S+1}{\Gamma}$ is the vector irrep $\Gamma$ with the spin $S$, a spin even under the inversion. Under spin--orbit coupling it splits into the irreps of $\grp{2C_{\infty h}}$ in
\begin{equation}\label{e:spinorbit}\restr{(\Gamma \irrprod \irrJp{S}{+1})}{\grp{2C_{\infty h}}},\end{equation}
and they are spinor irreps at half-integer $S$ and vector irreps at integer $S$. In a linear molecule they are the levels $\Omega = \lvert\Lambda + \Sigma\rvert$ of Hund's case (a), with $\Lambda$ and $\Sigma = -S, \dots, S$ the projections of the orbital and spin angular momenta on the axis. Row $\Gamma$, column $2S + 1$ of a table gives the levels of $\term{2S+1}{\Gamma}$ for the vector irreps of index at most 3 and $S \le \nicefrac{7}{2}$, and the record holds them (\texttt{spin\symbol{95}orbit\symbol{95}levels}). For an orbital level of section~\ref{s:spin_orbitals}, column $2S + 1 = 2$ gives the levels of one electron, and that section gives their states.

\needspace{91pt}
\section{\texorpdfstring{$jj$}{jj} configurations}\label{s:jj}
Conventions: \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf}{CONVENTIONS.pdf}, sections \href{../../../rotation/CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.19}{19} and \href{../../CONVENTIONS.pdf\#conv.25.3}{25.3}.
A spinor level is a Kramers level: a pseudoreal spinor irrep alone, a real one twice or a complex-conjugate pair together. In a level of dimension $d$, $n$ electrons fill its exterior power $\Lambda^{n}$, $n = 0, \dots, d$: vector irreps at even $n$ and spinor irreps at odd $n$. Every level of $\grp{2C_{\infty h}}$ has dimension 2: $\Lambda^{1}$ is the level, and $\Lambda^{0}$ and $\Lambda^{2}$ are the trivial irrep (\texttt{jj\symbol{95}configurations}).
